# Most Gracious Lord!

It is still fresh in my memory how Your Serene Highness <note>abbreviation: Ew. Durchl. (Eure Durchlaucht)</note>, the last time I traveled through those parts, deliberated with me on what might be the true and most direct path to reach the perfection of medicine. Whereupon I presented to Your Serene Highness the golden teaching of the keen-sighted philosopher **HELMONT** <note>Jan Baptist van Helmont (1580–1644) was a pioneering Flemish chemist and physician who bridged the gap between alchemy and early modern science.</note>: *namely, there are certain Sulfurs which, when corrected and perfected, the whole host of diseases obeys* <note>original Latin: "sunt videlicet Sulphura quædam, quibus correctis atque perfectis, tota morborum cohors auscultat"</note>. I not only proved the truth of this teaching regarding the *soul of antimony* <term>anima Antimonii: the refined medicinal essence extracted from the mineral antimony, believed by iatrochemists to be a universal cure</term> with strong arguments, but also—had my circumstances allowed me to stay longer—would have tangibly demonstrated it myself.

<summary>The author recalls a previous meeting with Prince Christian Augustus where they discussed the most effective medical treatments, specifically citing Van Helmont’s theories on the medicinal power of "Sulfurs" and antimony.</summary>

### The First Chapter.
## The Occasion for the Preparation of this Little Book.

<image-desc>A large decorative initial 'N' set within an ornamental square frame. The frame is topped with a crown and surrounded by stylized floral flourishes.</image-desc>
Since the great mercy of **God** has ignited a particularly bright light in **medicine** <term>Medicin: the art of healing, which in this era was shifting toward the use of chemical "arcana" or secrets</term> in this **century** <term>Seculo: from the Latin 'saeculum,' meaning an age or era</term> for the singular comfort of the sick through the highly gifted **Johann Baptist van Helmont** <note>Jan Baptist van Helmont (1580–1644) was a famous Flemish physician who argued that diseases should be treated with chemical remedies rather than the traditional herbal "humors" of Galenic medicine</note>, and since this light has been recognized and held in high esteem by so many hundreds of truth-seeking **physicians** <term>Medicis: practitioners of medicine</term>, and for that reason his profound writings left behind have been very diligently read and contemplated: yet, alas! it has come to this, that almost everyone complains about the obscurity <note>original: "Tunckelheit"; refers to the difficult, coded language often used by alchemists to hide their secrets from the unworthy</note>

# Concerning the Secret

...of the aforementioned books, particularly regarding the preparation of the high medicines, and above all else, regarding the **tincture** or **sulfur** from **copper**, which everyone has judged—rightly so—to be the most necessary thing in medicine. This is why it has come to pass that through extensive correspondence, one friend has sought an explanation of this secret from another, and everyone has been concerned to the highest degree about this golden **arcanum** <term>Arcanum: a deep secret or a powerful medical "remedy" hidden in nature</term>, a "sickness" <note>The author uses the metaphor of a sickness to describe the intense, almost obsessive desire to solve alchemical mysteries</note> with which I myself was afflicted for a long time.

After I had diligently read our philosopher's <note>Referring to Jan Baptist van Helmont, mentioned on the previous page</note> instructive books and yet could form no concept from them of how to reach the unknotting of this secret, I decided within myself to learn this by seeking out wise and understanding people. Therefore, I spared no effort in travel, but sought out experienced people in this secret art here and there in various kingdoms, often even at great risk to my life. I conferred with them about this highly important treasure of health, but could find none who could release for me this firmly bound **Sulfur of the Philosophers** <note>original: "Sulphur Philosophorum"; in alchemy, sulfur represents the active, masculine, or soul-like principle of a substance</note>, or give me even the slightest information. Indeed, even among the most famous, I found not a few who could not cleanly separate the **soul** <note>original: "animam"; referring to the vital essence or "form" of a mineral</note> even from **antimony** <term>Antimony: a metallic element often used in alchemical medicine for purging and purification</term>, let alone from copper. Just as one who has once been ill knows best how a sick person feels—and thus it is not unknown to me with what great longing the ignorant and the straying have sought and awaited this **Philosophers' Sulfur** <note>original: *Sulphur Philosophorum*; the active, combustible, and soul-like principle that alchemists sought to extract from metals</note>—I considered it a duty of Christian love to share with my fellow brothers that which I have perceived (next to the grace of God) through much effort, time, and expense. I wish to clearly present to them the path I have walked and how I arrived at this knowledge, so that they may follow this small, kindled light, perceive this secret by its glow, and also arrive at its unfolding <note>original: *Aufwickelung*; literally the "unrolling" or "unknotting" of a complex problem</note>. In this way, they may be able to do much good for their neighbor in his sickness, so that through many, the great name of God may be praised in His wonders, and much praise be born unto Him as the source of all good gifts.

## The Second Chapter.

### Explaining the Power by which a mineral **Soul** <note>original: *Anima*; in alchemy, the vital, internal spark or essence of a substance</note> or **Sulfur** <note>original: *Sulphur*; the active, combustible principle of a metal</note> must be separated from its Body.

If we wish to thoroughly understand by what means the soul must be separated from hard-**coagulated** <note>original: *coagulirten*; refers to the process of a liquid or spiritual essence becoming a solid body</note> metallic bodies, such as Copper, Gold, Iron, etc., we must first investigate how not only common sulfur, but also the **sulfurs** <note>original: *Sulphura*</note> of minerals—which are only half-coagulated bodies—are dissolved. By understanding this thoroughly, we may climb higher as if by steps, and thus be assisted in the separation of those much more firmly coagulated sulfurs. For because all metallic and mineral sulfurs have entirely the same nature, and are called the **element of fire** <note>original: *Element des Feuers*</note> by the **Philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophis*; a term for natural scientists and alchemists</note>, and are of a fiery nature, it must necessarily follow that their dissolution arises from a single foundation. Since they are of one essence and nature in their origin, it is no different than how an old man and a newborn child are of entirely one essence, only that one is more perfect than the other. Therefore, if we wish to find the dissolution of the firmly coagulated metallic sulfurs,

<summary>This chapter establishes a foundational alchemical principle: that the "soul" or sulfur of all metals is identical in nature, differing only in its degree of solidification or "coagulation." The author argues that by learning to extract sulfur from softer minerals, one can eventually learn to unlock the secrets of hard metals like gold and iron.</summary>

<keywords>alchemy, sulfur, anima, coagulation, metallic soul, element of fire, dissolution, minerals, gold, copper, iron</keywords>

### Of the Sulfur of the Philosophers.

<meta>catchword: out</meta>

wish to investigate, first, on what ground and by what means the lesser **sulfurs** <note>original: *Sulphura*</note> are unlocked; for in such a manner I have traced the truth, and thus have I found it. Thus, when we inquire first as to what thoroughly unlocks common sulfur, we find that oil and everything of a fatty and fiery nature dissolves sulfur as its equal—for it is also a fiery being—and places its otherwise hidden blood-red **soul** <note>original: *animam*</note> before our eyes. Although this is a common thing and is **practiced** <note>original: *practicirt*</note> daily by many, it is nevertheless of such depth and importance that from this foundation flows the dissolution of all metallic souls, even of Gold itself, as you will hear hereafter. 

Therefore, if we wish to be disciples of the **Philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophorum*</note>, we must look upon sulfur with quite different eyes than the common man when it is softened by boiling with an oil in such a manner that its innermost red **tincture** <note>original: *Tinctur*; the concentrated essence or spiritual "dye" of a substance</note> is turned outward. The common crowd is satisfied that, when such a boiling is completed, it can take away the **itch** <note>original: *Kratze*; likely referring to scabies or a common skin ailment</note>, heal wounds, and restore bruised or burned limbs, and perform other healing works. But a searcher of secret wisdom goes deeper to the foundation and asks: how does it happen that oil, among all things, alone has such power that it dissolves sulfur so gently and naturally and [brings] its internal [essence]...

<summary>The author explains that common sulfur can be dissolved by oil to reveal its "blood-red soul," a process that serves as a foundational lesson for extracting the essences of higher metals like gold. # Of the Secret

outward, even though the strongest **corrosive** <term>an acidic or caustic substance used in alchemy to break down materials</term> cannot achieve this. If we only perceive this basis correctly, we find that this **dissolution** <note>original: *Auflöſung*</note> arises from the equality and shared essence of the oil and the sulfur. Namely, that the oil or fatness, as a fiery substance, desires, seizes, embraces, softens, and dissolves the sulfur—being also a fiery and thus similar essence—down to its very innermost **center** <note>original: *Centrum*</note>. 

This occurs in a **sympathetic manner** <term>based on the alchemical principle of "like attracts like," where substances with similar spiritual signatures act upon one another</term>, as like loves its like, and one communicates its power to the other. Thus, oil, as a fiery moisture, is the true foundation and main source from which the **unlocking** <note>original: *Aufſchlieſſung*</note> of all mineral and metallic **sulfurs** <note>original: *Sulphuren*</note> flows forth. 

If we proceed further from common sulfur to the other sulfurs of minerals—which have already undergone a considerably hard **coagulation** <note>the process of thickening or hardening into a solid state</note> through the **cooking of nature** <term>the natural subterranean heat believed by alchemists to mature minerals into metals over long periods of time</term>—and compare them against the **experience** <note>original: *Experienz*</note> just mentioned, then an oil indeed has no power to separate and dissolve an **embryonated sulfur** <term>a "fetal" or immature sulfur trapped within a raw mineral ore</term> of any mineral, because it is much too weak. 

Nevertheless, this dissolution must also flow from this same foundation if it is to be natural, kind, gentle, and thorough. However, because minerals are already firmly locked away and do not yield to such a mild power as oil, the power of the **oily essence** <note>original: *ölichten Weſens*</note> must be heightened in its fieriness, multiplied, and made much more fiery still. Then it possesses the power that...

### Of the Sulfur of the Philosophers.

...it dissolves the *Sulfur* <note>original: *Sulphur*</note> from the *Minerals* just as easily as oil has the power to soften common sulfur—indeed, even much sooner and faster. However, such an elevation of the fiery *quality* and power in oils must happen in no other way than according to nature; that is, it must occur through its own kind, if we wish to work according to nature and not against it. This is then accomplished by an ignited fire, which through its fiery and violent power makes and transforms the oily essence into a very fiery salt. This salt not only retains its previous oily and softening power in every way but is also brought to an incomparably higher degree in both its fiery and softening power. 

For when wood, which is full of oil, is thrown into the fire and burned, the largest part of the oil is indeed consumed by the fire—since the flame and heat arise from its consumption—but at the same time, a large portion which cannot burn so quickly (due to the obstruction and fusion of the volatile salt <term>a salt that easily evaporates or sublimes when heated</term>, which is also in the wood alongside the oil) is transformed by the sharp and violent power of the ignited fire into a very fiery, fixed <term>stable in fire; does not evaporate or change when heated</term>, oily salt. This is called an *alkali* <note>original: *alcali*</note> or a lye-salt <note>original: *Laugen-Saltz*</note>, from which the common man makes his lye; and this salt still perfectly possesses its previous softening, oily property, only that through the fire it has been changed from a volatile into a fixed...

<meta>catchword: To-</meta>

<summary>This page explains how the alchemist creates a powerful solvent for minerals by transforming "oily essences" into "fiery salts" (alkalis) through fire. Using the analogy of burning wood to create lye, the author describes how fire "fixes" the volatile components into a stable, potent substance that retains its softening properties.</summary>

<keywords>Sulfur of the Philosophers, alkali, lye-salt, fixed salt, volatile salt, oily essence, mineral dissolution, alchemical fire</keywords>

# Of the Secret

...state, but has become much more fiery and powerful than before. So, if you wish to know whether this fiery salt still possesses a true oily essence, simply place a little common sulfur along with this salt into a pot, add a little water, and let it boil briefly. Then the sulfur will dissolve <note>original: *zerſchmelzen*</note> within it and release its red tincture <term>the concentrated essence or "color" of a substance, believed by alchemists to hold its medicinal or transformative power</term>, just as if it had occurred using an oil, but much more rapidly. This is because the salt is now incomparably more powerful in its softening fieriness than it was while it was still an oil.

Just as such a fiery salt is nothing other than a fiery oil greatly enhanced in its potency, it likewise possesses the power to act more forcefully and to dissolve <note>original: *aufflöſen*</note> that which was impossible for it to accomplish while it was still merely an oil. With this strength, it has the power to break down minerals <note>original: *Mineralia*</note>, to separate their soul or *Sulfur* <note>original: *Sulphur*; in alchemy, sulfur often represents the soul or the flammable, active principle of a substance</note>, and to soften them through dissolution.

To perform this even better, more easily, and more quickly, the fieriness of such a fiery *alkali* <note>original: *alcali*</note> can be further increased if it is mixed with other fiery substances of its own kind and strongly burned, thereby intensifying its fiery nature. One such fiery substance is unslaked lime <term>also known as quicklime; a caustic substance produced by heating limestone</term>, which acquires its great heat through the intense power of the burning fire and can, in turn, impart this heat to the *alkalis* <note>original: *Alalien*, likely a scribal error for *Alkalien*</note> through the fire. Therefore, take a well-purified lye-salt <note>original: *Laugſaltz*</note>, the *Salt of Tartar* <note>original: *Sal tartari*; a high-quality alkali made from the sediment of wine barrels</note>, which is the most fiery...

<summary>This page explains how to test if a "fiery salt" retains its oily properties by using it to dissolve sulfur and release its red tincture. It further describes how to enhance the salt's solvent power by combining it with unslaked lime to better extract the "souls" of minerals.</summary>

<keywords>alkali, sulfur, salt of tartar, unslaked lime, tincture, dissolution, minerals, lye-salt, alchemy</keywords>

--- Page 21 ---
<meta>This page continues from page 14, completing the sentence regarding how the fiery salt can impart heat even without the presence of actual fire.</meta>

# Of the Sulfur of the Philosophers

...without that is, and mix it with lime <term>unslaked lime or quicklime; a caustic substance produced by heating limestone</term>, let it calcine <term>heat to a high temperature to cause chemical change</term> strongly for a day, and afterwards leach it out again. In this way, you have greatly increased your alkali in its fieriness, which then has the power to very quickly dissolve the sulfur <note>original: *Sulphur*; in alchemy, sulfur represents the active, flammable principle or "soul" of a substance</note> from the minerals and release it from its body. You can experience this for yourself, for example, with Antimony <note>original: *Antimonio*</note>, as it is the most noble of the minerals. When you grind it as small as dust and add the aforementioned fiery salt to it, pour in some water, and let it stand in a warm place for several days, stirring it often during the day, then such a fiery alkali draws the sulfur to itself through sympathetic properties <note>the alchemical belief that substances with natural affinities attract one another</note>, dissolves it, and unites with it. 

Then one pours off all the moisture quite cleanly, or filters it, and pours some sharp substances <note>likely acidic liquids like vinegar or spirits of salt used to precipitate the dissolved matter</note> into it; then the sulfur of antimony falls to the bottom, in the shape and color of the most beautiful orange <note>original: *Pomerantze*; referring to the bitter orange</note>, and burns just like any other sulfur—yet that which falls last is the purest and best. Through these two steps <note>original: *Staffeln*; literally rungs of a ladder</note> now—namely, through the dissolution of common sulfur in oils, and through the separation of embryonic sulfurs <term>sulfur still trapped in its crude mineral state, like a fetus in a womb</term> from the minerals in a fiery alkali—I have ascended higher, as if on certain steps, toward the release <note>original: *Entbindung*; also implying a "birthing" or delivery</note> of the metallic sulfurs, because they are of one essence with the mineral sulfurs. And thus, I have recognized in a clear mirror through the Light of Nature <note>a Paracelsian term for the innate wisdom found in the natural world through divine inspiration</note> that, just as the oil was not powerful enough...

...to separate a sulfur <term>original: *Sulphur*; the active, combustible principle or "soul" of a substance</term> from the minerals, but that such an oil must first have been placed into a much higher degree of fieriness before it could do such a thing; likewise, it is the same with the metallic sulfurs. Because these are much more firmly bound to their mercurial bodies <term>the fluid, passive principle of matter; the "body" of the metal</term> and have entered into a much harder coagulation <term>the process of a liquid becoming solid</term> than the minerals, a much more penetrating fire is required for this than the fixed alkalis <term>alkaline salts that resist the heat of fire</term> are for the minerals. Yet, because the sulfurs are of one essence, this dissolution must nevertheless flow from this same foundation. Nothing else is lacking here, except that one makes this fiery salt even more penetrating, even more softening, even more vehement, and even more powerful. 

This would then have to be obtained if one were to release such very fiery salts and alkalis from their coarse physicality, drive them up through the strong power of fire, and turn them into a penetrating, fiery, volatile spirit <term>a substance that has been purified and vaporized into a gaseous or highly subtle state</term>. This spirit would not only still possess the same nature, fieriness, and power as before, but would have become much more penetrating and much more softening, because it has been freed from all coarse bonds, transformed into a completely spiritual, very fiery, volatile water, and thus has acquired a powerfully softening property. Following this insight of mine, I then put it into practice, and I succeeded, so that the sulfurs of the metals willingly surrendered themselves to such a fiery volatile spirit, and [yielded] all their power to the—

Nor should it seem too strange to anyone that I have said that even gold itself must surrender its blood-red soul or sulfur to this fiery, penetrating spirit; for experience has taught me this, and proves it daily, despite the fact that the otherwise deeply learned Cosmopolite Philosopher <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous and influential alchemist who wrote under the pseudonym "Cosmopolita"</note> in the preface of his *Twelve Treatises* <note>original: *12. tractatuum*; referring to his famous work "De Lapide Philosophorum"</note> maintains the contrary <note>original: *contrarium ſtatuirt*</note>, as do many others today who consider it impossible.

# Of the Secret

...recognize, not truly for its great power and virtue alone, with which it is endowed, but also because of its secret and difficult preparation, which the sages kept only among themselves and held in great secrecy. It has remained secret until this time, and will likely remain secret and hidden from all those who love gold more than God, even though I shall make it as clear as anyone has done before; for God always holds His hand over His secrets and does not let the swine partake of noble pearls. As our *Philosopher of Fevers* <note>Likely referring to Jan Baptist van Helmont (1580–1644), a Flemish physician who wrote a famous treatise on fevers and was a hero to "chemical" doctors</note> testifies in chapter 14, section 9, saying:

> In few words I have completed the secret that ennobles the physician; but to have prepared it for the first time is a work of immense labor, and its guidance depends on the hand of Him to whom all honor is due, because He reveals such secrets to the little ones which the world does not know, and therefore disdains. <note>original: *Paucis absolvi secretum, quod medicum nobilitat: at istud parasse pro prima vice est ingentis operae, pendetque ejus directio è manu ejus, cui debetur omnis honos, quia parvulis revelat ejusmodi arcana quae mundus nescit, & idcirco vilipendit.*</note>

Therefore, you must not think that it is such a simple and easy matter to obtain the golden <term>tincture</term> <note>in alchemy, a potent liquid extract or "soul" of a metal believed to have transformative properties</note> of copper. For not only must the <term>dry solvent</term> <note>original: *menstruum sic*; a specialized substance used to dissolve solids or extract their essence</note> draw out and take up the soul—namely, the alkali made volatile—but the preparation of the copper itself involves very extraordinary and hitherto unknown secrets, about which all self-proclaimed physicians know nothing at all. For first, the copper must be turned into a <term>vitriol</term> <note>a metallic sulfate crystal; copper vitriol is typically bright blue</note>, not in the common, unknown way—for that has no place here—but in a much more secret manner, "by a far more hidden path" <note>original: *via longe ocultiori*</note>, as our Philosopher says.

Of the Secret

## The Fourth Chapter.

### Reports how the Vitriol of the Philosophers is made from Copper.

Although in the extraction of the *soul* <note>original: *animæ*</note> from copper, the greatest and most noble part is the volatile <term>alkali</term> <note>A basic chemical substance, here rendered into a vaporous or "spirit" state to act as a solvent</note>, yet this alone is not sufficient; for even if it is poured onto <term>calcined</term> <note>Reduced to a powder through high heat</note> copper (just as I initially proceeded, but in vain), it cannot separate its golden soul from it. This is because, through the hard <term>coagulation</term> <note>The process of hardening or solidifying into a physical metal</note> performed by nature, as well as the melting fire that follows it, the *soul of copper* <note>original: *anima cupri*</note> is locked within death, and thus turned entirely inward. In this sense, the <term>Cosmopolite</term> <note>The pseudonym of Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist known for his theories on the "food of life" in the air</note> very rightly said, *that the melting of metals is their death* <note>original: *quod fusio metallorum sit eorum mors*</note>. And our Philosopher <note>Van Helmont</note> in *On the Stone* <note>original: *de lithiasi*</note>, chapter 8, section 4, says: *while it is being melted, all medical power is enclosed, sealed, and indeed thus turned inward,* etc. Therefore, the soul must be turned outward again, and the melted metal must, as it were, rise again from death. For this reason, our Philosopher also calls it the *rising and glorious sulfur* <note>original: *Sulphur resurgens & gloriosum*</note>, as it is freed from all its hard, bodily bonds. From this it is clear that such a preparation of copper is no less necessary—indeed, no less important—than the preparation of the extracting, fiery, volatile *spirit*...

# Of the Sulphur of the Philosophers.

The spirit itself—which is why it is always a solvent of the same kind—was kept entirely secret by the Philosophers, until our faithful and well-meaning Philosopher <note>Referring to Jan Baptist van Helmont</note> helped us onto the trail by reporting to us that such preparation or "making-ready" of the copper must be performed through <term>sal ammoniac</term> <note>original: *Salmiac*; ammonium chloride, a salt used in alchemy to make metals volatile or to create "secret" solvents</note>. Concerning this, his teaching in his treatise titled *Duumviratus* <note>A work by Van Helmont concerning the "two rulers" of the human body, the stomach and spleen, but often containing alchemical secrets</note>, section 9, reads as follows:

> For that which is legitimate and which they call the **Sulphur of the Philosophers** is made from the green spirit of vitriol, which—through repeated <term>cohobation</term> <note>The process of redistilling a liquid by pouring it back onto the solid matter left in the flask</note>, expressed by the ultimate torture of fire and made remarkably volatile—is coagulated and fixed; this is performed by common sal ammoniac, which must thereafter be carried away from it through repeated distillations of <term>spirit of wine</term> <note>Concentrated alcohol</note>. <note>original Latin: *Fit enim legitimum & quod Philosophorum (Sulphur) vocant, è spiritu viridi vitrioli, qui per repetitam cohobationem, ultima ignis tortura expressus & insigniter volatilis, coagulatur atque fixatur, quod præstat vulgaris sal armeniacus, qui inde postmodum aufferri debet per spiritus vini repetitas destillationes.*</note>

Because this is then supposed to happen through the sal ammoniac, you might perhaps think that it must be put to work using the substance as it is in itself—just as I also initially held such thoughts, and many times "elevated" <note>An alchemical term for causing a substance to rise as vapor</note> the copper through <term>sublimation</term> <note>The process of heating a solid until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again, used for purification</note> with the sal ammoniac, but all in vain. After much futile effort, I began to take better note of our Philosopher's far-reaching words, where he teaches that the <term>vitriol</term> <note>A sulfate of metal; here, copper sulfate</note> of copper should be made into a spirit that is not merely ordinary, but very volatile indeed. Therefore, the sal ammoniac itself—since it is meant to perform this task—must first be made into a very volatile spirit; for how indeed could the copper be able to become a very volatile spirit if that which promotes such...

# Of the Secret

For such an attempt is just as futile as if one were to take filed or <term>calcined</term> <note>Metal that has been roasted into a powder</note> copper; indeed, the spirits of vitriol (or any other corrosive) that have turned the copper into vitriol are not only useless for this work, but are also harmful and obstructive. So much so, that if someone—following the teaching of our Philosopher <note>Referring again to Jan Baptist van Helmont</note>—wished to extract the **tincture** or **sulphur** from the naturally grown or artificially made common blue vitriol of copper, they would first have to dissolve the vitriol in water, precipitate the metallic essence with an **alkali** <note>A basic substance used to neutralize the acid and make the metal "fall out" of the liquid</note>, and free it entirely and completely from its corrosive [acid], so that not the slightest bit remains. 

Afterward, one must take the precipitated and dried **calx of the metal** <note>original: *Kalck des Metalls*; the powdery oxide or purified residue of the metal</note> and turn it into a vitriol once again using the **spirit of sal ammoniac**; otherwise, nothing can be attained. # Of the Sulphur of the Philosophers

...**sal ammoniac** <note>original: *Salmiac*; ammonium chloride</note> must first be transformed into a volatile **spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus*; a vaporous or liquid essence</note>, the reader must not think that this occurs in a common or well-known manner, or that such a **method** <note>original: *modus*</note> has been known until now. One should not, as some do, distill the sal ammoniac with **flour**, as those people quite erroneously and incorrectly mistake the resulting sour water for the **spirit** of the sal ammoniac. This liquid is actually nothing more than the acidity of the flour mixed with a few tiny particles of the sal ammoniac; soon after, the entire sal ammoniac follows and rises in its original weight. Indeed, every vegetable material yields such a sour spirit when it is distilled.

Furthermore, that fiery, **urinous salt** <note>Ammonium carbonate, often called "volatile salt of urine"</note> which is made with **salt of tartar** <note>original: *Sale tartari*; potassium carbonate</note> or another **alkali**, is not the same **spirit** which the **Philosophers** <note>Alchemists</note> require for this work. This is because that salt is only the smallest part of the sal ammoniac; the greatest part remains at the bottom. Therefore, this salt is incorrectly called a **spirit** of sal ammoniac. Since a thing—as is the case with sal ammoniac—consists of two parts, the naming of it always and primarily belongs to the largest part, as the saying goes: **a thing is named after its greater part** <note>original: *à potiori parte fit denominatio*</note>. 

Experience proves this: if one mixes a pound of sal ammoniac with an **alkali**, approximately 6 or 7 **Loth** <note>A historical Germanic unit of weight; 32 Loth typically equaled one pound</note> of the urinous salt (which is truly no spirit, but only a volatile salt) will rise up, while more than 24 Loth remain at the bottom with the alkali. To those 24 Loth, as...

### Of the Mystery

...to the largest part; indeed, the name **sal ammoniac** <note>original: *Salmiac*; ammonium chloride</note> belongs more to this than to the 7 **loth** <note>A German unit of weight; 7 loth is approximately 110 grams or 3.8 ounces</note> that rose up. For this reason, the largest part remaining at the bottom is what the **Sages** <note>The Alchemists</note> have always understood and intended, and from it, a **volatile spirit** <note>original: *spiritus*; a vaporous essence</note> was driven for their purpose. Experience has taught me this, as I have seen that the aforementioned **urinous salt** <note>Ammonium carbonate, which has a strong ammonia smell</note> is of no use in this matter and could not bring to light what the Sages desire. I have therefore sought it in the largest part and found that, specifically, if one takes this aforementioned largest part—which remains at the bottom with an **alkali** <note>Likely potassium carbonate or salt of tartar</note> after the separation of the urinous salt—and distills it with a very strong fire according to the **Art** <note>The "Art" refers to the specific techniques of alchemy</note>, one obtains not only a very volatile, but also a **Double Spirit**. I call it very volatile because it even rises in a **water bath** <note>original: *Balneo* (Bain-marie); a gentle heat</note> and can thus make other **bodies** <note>original: *Cörper* or *corpora*; chemical substances</note> very volatile as well. It is also "double" because, as the urinous salt separated from it, it reunited with the alkali, from which it cannot be separated except by a very intense fire. When this separation occurs, it carries a portion of the alkali over with it because it had united so intimately with it that it cannot pass over without it. Thus, a double or **saturated spirit** is created, which has satisfied its biting, **corrosive nature** <note>original: *corrosivischen Natur*</note> upon the alkali; because of this, it no longer corrodes other bodies nor seeks to penetrate them, because it

...already, as much as its **corrosive** <term>corrosive</term> <note>original: *corrosiv*; a substance used to dissolve metals</note> required, it is already saturated by the **alkali** <term>alkali</term>, wherefore this doubled and saturated, very volatile **spirit** <term>spirit</term> <note>original: *spiritus*; the vaporous or volatile essence of a substance</note> is of a wonderful nature. It dissolves metallic **bodies** <note>original: *Cörper*; substances or elements</note> in a completely different way than other corrosives, inasmuch as common **corrosives** <term>corrosives</term> <note>original: *corrosiva*; such as strong acids like nitric or sulfuric acid</note> have the nature that—because they are hungry and eager to satisfy their corrosiveness upon the bodies—they attack the metals with fury and effervescence, eating away at them and clinging to them from the outside.

This doubled **corrosive**, however, because it is already saturated, does not eat away at the metal, nor does it seek a dwelling place within it like others do, but rather softens it. It does not cling to it from the outside, nor does it perform its action quickly like the others, but gradually over several days. It penetrates to the very innermost part of the metal and turns outward what is locked and sealed in the internal **center** <term>center</term> <note>original: *Centro*; the essential core or "heart" of the matter</note>. This occurs through the power of its doubled nature, in which the metal melts like ice in water.

Therefore, apply diligence so that you make this **spirit** from the **sal ammoniac** <term>sal ammoniac</term> <note>original: *Salmiac*; ammonium chloride</note>, and do not be deterred by the fact that much labor belongs to it and special attention is required; rather, think of the end result—that this will gladden you in return, instead of it having been somewhat difficult for you to prepare. If you hit upon this and do it correctly, then you have done the greatest and most noble part of the matter, and the rest will be but a game to you. Take, therefore, your very volatile **spirit**,

...how another doubled **spirit** <note>original: *spiritus*; in alchemy, a volatile substance that can be distilled.</note> could be made? Regarding this, you should know that this occurs solely and alone because of the **spirit**, or rather the **salt of urine** <note>original: *salis urinæ*; urea or ammonia compounds derived from urine</note>, from which **sal ammoniac** <term>Sal ammoniac</term> <note>original: *Salmiac*; ammonium chloride. A key substance used by alchemists to make minerals volatile.</note> consists. This **salt of urine** always retains the nature of a salt and cannot be made into a true **spirit**. Therefore, it also carries up the **spirit of salt** <note>original: *spiritum salis*; hydrochloric acid</note>, **vitriol** <note>original: *vitrioli*; sulfuric acid</note>, and other **corrosives** (which are true spirits) not in the form of a spirit, but according to its own nature as a salt. Indeed, by its salty nature, it even turns those spirits into salt or transforms them into a salty form as long as it is mixed with such spirits.

Now, however, our **Philosopher** <note>Likely Jan Baptist van Helmont (1580–1644), a pivotal figure who bridged the gap between alchemy and early chemistry, as the titles cited here are his works.</note>, as well as **Paracelsus** <note>The famous 16th-century Swiss-German physician who revolutionized alchemy by focusing on medicine.</note> and others before him, desires that **copper** <note>original: *Kupffer*</note> should not pass over as a volatile green salt, but as a very volatile green **spirit**. And in *The Duumvirate* <note>original: *Duumviratu*; another work by Van Helmont</note>, he says that the **green spirit** <note>original: *spiritus viridis*</note> should be **remarkably volatile** <note>original: *insigniter volatilis*</note>. 

This is done solely so that the **body** <note>original: *corpus*</note> of the copper may be rightly prepared and made suitable to release its **tincture** <note>original: *Tinctur*; the essential color-principle or "soul" of a substance</note> afterward. Therefore, the metal must be so violently torn apart and reduced into the smallest dust-motes—as if into a subtle spirit—for otherwise, if this does not happen, no **soul** <note>original: *anima*</note> can be obtained. As he says in express words in the place already cited from *On Lithiasis*, Section 5: **"Moreover, the fire of Venus cannot be had except with the full destruction of the copper and of its mercurial body..."** <note>original: *Non potest autem haberi ignis veneris, nisi cum plenaria (NB). cupri destructione, corporisque mercurialis ipsius*; 'Venus' is the alchemical name for copper, and its 'fire' refers to its inner sulfur or energy.</note>

### Of the Sulphur of the Philosophers.
<header>Of the Sulphur of the Philosophers.</header>

...to bring **copper** <note>original: *Kupffer*</note> into a volatile **spirit** <note>original: *spiritum*; a vaporous or highly refined substance</note> is not useful [using common methods]; therefore, they chose instead such a fiery salt which, when it is distilled, becomes a true **spirit**, and thus never rises in the form of a salt, much less transforms the **corrosive** <note>original: *corrosiv*; usually a strong acid like nitric or hydrochloric acid used to dissolve metals</note> added to it into the form of a dry salt (as the **salt of urine** <term>Salt of urine</term> <note>original: *Sal urinæ*; ammonium carbonate. The author is comparing how different substances react with acids.</note> does). To discover such a thing, they observed the property and power of the fiery **salt of urine**, namely that its nature is to "kill" or saturate all **corrosives** <note>original: *corrosiva*</note>. Because of this power, it **precipitates** <note>original: *präcipitirt*; causes a dissolved solid to fall out of a liquid solution</note> the **metals** <note>original: *Metalla*</note> that are dissolved in a **corrosive**, because it greedily attacks the **corrosive**, saturates itself with it, and thus causes the eroded metal to fall down. And because they found this same power—and indeed an even mightier one—in the fiery, fixed **alkalis** <note>original: *Acalien* or *Alcalia*; alkaline salts like potash.</note>, they recognized these **alkalis** to be useful for this purpose. Just as in the preparation of **sal ammoniac** <note>original: *Salmiacs*</note> the fieriness of the **salt of urine** saturates the **corrosive**, so too in their undertaking do the **alkalis** receive the **corrosive**, and from the two of them a **third, doubled, saturated salt** <note>original: *tertium, duplicatum, satiatum sal*</note> should arise, and **consequently** <note>original: *consequenter*</note> through **distillation** <note>original: *destillation*</note> a similarly doubled **spirit** should spring forth. They also previously distilled the **alkalis** for the first time through a very strong fire, according to the requirements of the art, and found that they rose up not as a volatile salt, but as a true, very subtle and penetrating **spirit** (possessing great and mighty power and virtue in **medicine** <note>original: *Medicin*</note>). Therefore, they [treated] the **sal ammoniac** with the **alkalis**...

Since both the **corrosive** and the **alkalis** can be sublimated into a true **spirit** <note>original: *spiritu*; a volatile, vaporous essence</note> in a strong fire, it must inevitably and necessarily follow that from this doubled salt, nothing other than a true, saturated, and doubled **spirit** must result.

For through its twofold and volatile power, it penetrates to the innermost **center** of every **atom** <term>Atom</term> <note>original: *atomi*; in this context, the smallest fundamental particles of a metal</note> of the metals. It turns outward all the power, color, **tincture** <note>original: *Tinctur*; the concentrated essence or soul of a substance</note>, and ability that lay hidden within and as if dead, making them living and active once again.

For although the corrosive in sal ammoniac flows from salt according to its first origin, yet—because of the union it underwent with the fiery **salt of urine** <note>ammonium salts derived from urine</note>, where one acted upon the other and changed each other's nature—it has acquired another and much more excellent nature than it had before. Therefore, the **philosophers** had to stay with sal ammoniac and seek their corrosive from it.

And I must confess that after I learned to understand and know the ground and root of sal ammoniac, I also hit upon the thought that one might well use **spirit of salt** for it; I even put this into practice, but with a great difference: I obtained no such **spirit** as that from sal ammoniac. ### The Sixth Chapter.

## How the sweet vitriol of copper must be distilled into a green spirit.

Since it became known from the writings left behind by the **Philosophers** <term>Philosophorum</term> <note>original: *Philosophorum*; referring to the historical alchemists and natural philosophers</note> that a **green spirit** <term>spiritus</term> <note>original: *spiritus*; a volatile, distilled essence</note> is to be made from **vitriol** <term>Vitriol</term> <note>a sulfate of certain metals; in this context, likely copper or iron sulfate</note> and **copper** <term>Kupffer</term>, and that this spirit possesses very great powers, the eager seekers of truth have at all times exerted themselves to the highest degree to obtain this noble green spirit and to rejoice in its power and virtue. 

However, because they saw that the vitriol—no matter how much they treated it through **distilling** <term>destilliren</term> and re-distilling—would not grant them such a green spirit as promised by the Philosophers, they came to the realization that the ancient sages did not wish to be understood as obtaining it through common methods of distillation. Instead, they surmised that such **greenness** <term>Grünigkeit</term> must be obtained in a particularly subtle way or through specific, unknown manual techniques. Therefore, they began to devise all sorts of ways to achieve their purpose. 

At first, there were some who, in order to successfully obtain the greenness, distilled the most subtle **spirit of vitriol** <term>spiritum vitrioli</term> over various **vegetable substances** <term>Vegetabilien</term> so many times that the repeated **cohobation** <term>cohobation</term> <note>the process of repeatedly distilling a liquid over its own solid residue to concentrate its essence</note> carried the greenness of the herbs over with it, and have thus [with a]...

...comforted by a green color borrowed from vegetables, and thereby quieted their eager minds; however, this substance possessed only as much power as the strengths of the added herbs allowed. Others, seeing that the greenness of **vitriol** <term>Vitriol</term> <note>original: *Vitrioli*; a metal sulfate, usually of copper or iron, valued for its medicinal and chemical properties</note> would not rise upward and over the top of the distilling vessel, reversed the process and drove it downwards. With great rejoicing, they believed they had grasped the truth of the matter. Yet, in such a process, a portion of the **vitriol** <note>original: *Vitrioli*</note> falls through along with the rest, which necessarily turns the moisture green. Others followed who recognized that this **method** <note>original: *modus*</note> of driving the substance downward was far too crude. They devised something that had the appearance of a special, so-called "philosophical distillation" and a unique, secret, uncommon philosophical **vitriol**. They claimed that the **Philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophi*; referring to the ancient alchemical authorities</note> did not use common vitriol or copper for this work, but rather took their own material, unknown until now, which they created from the **seed of all metals** <term>Samen aller Metallen</term>. This "seed," they claimed, was a type of round stone found in the richest mineral veins <note>original: *fettesten Betten*; literally "fattest beds," referring to layers of earth or clay rich in minerals</note>. Just as this was a special **matter** <note>original: *Materia*</note> for a secret vitriol, it also had to be distilled in a secret and special way, rather than by common methods...

<summary>The author critiques alchemists who use herbal dyes or crude downward distillation to fake a "green spirit," noting that poorly purified vitriol causes violent vomiting. He introduces a more sophisticated theory involving a "secret vitriol" derived from a "seed of metals" found in mineral-rich earth.</summary>

This page addresses those who claim that simple atmospheric moisture absorption and filtration constitute the "secret distillation" of the Sages.</meta>

<page-num>46</page-num>
<header># Of the Secret</header>

...instead, after this seed has impregnated itself with sufficient moisture from the air so that it can dissolve into water, they have purified such a resulting green metallic juice from its corporeality through filtration. They have interpreted this filtering as if it were the distillation of the **Philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophorum*</note>. In doing so, they have not only done great violence to the word "distill" and forced it to mean "filter," but have also **advertised** <note>original: *venditirt*; to sell or promote a concept</note> their own invented **prime matter** <note>original: *materiam primam*</note> or **seed of metals** <note>original: *semen metallorum*</note> (which is actually called by its right name **pyrite** <term>pyrites</term> <note>original: *pyrites*; iron disulfide, often called "fool's gold," which produces green vitriol when oxidized in moist air</note>, and proves itself to be so in reality) as the **womb of the vitriol of the philosophers** <note>original: *matricem vitrioli philosophorum*</note>. 

However, experience testifies that such a supposed philosophical vitriol does not have the nature, quality, and property that the true **Vitriol** <note>original: *Vitriolum*</note> of the Sages has—namely, that it is sweet and brings sleep, as a true **dwelling place of the true pain-reliever of the philosophers** <note>original: *domicilium veri anodyni philosophorum*</note>. Rather, this invented vitriol of the **Philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophorum*</note> is just as coarse, violent, bitter, gruesome, and strange as if it were a common one—as indeed it is nothing other than common vitriol, because it has the same origin as the common sort: namely, from **flint** <term>Feuerstein</term> <note>original: *Feuerstein*; likely referring to the sulfide ores found alongside flint or the spark-producing quality of pyrite</note>. 

And just as this supposed **philosophical vitriol** <note>original: *vitr. phil.*</note> is false, so too is the **promoted** <note>original: *venditirte*</note> distillation—namely, **filtration** <note>original: *filtratio*</note>—a mere invented pretense and a deliberate distortion of the words of the Sages. This distorted philosophical distillation (otherwise called **filtration** <note>original: *filtratio*</note>) gives us an even more intense greenness...

### Of the Sulphur of the Philosophers
<note>original: *des Sulphuris Philosophorum*</note>

...than this one, which is prepared **by descent** <term>per descensum: a distillation method where the heat is applied from above, forcing the substance to melt or vaporize and flow downward through a grate or filter into a receiver</term>, because here not all of the **Vitriol** <note>original: *Vitriol*; metallic sulfates, usually of iron or copper</note> yet passes through or falls down, but only a very small amount. Whereas there, in that inverted philosophical distillation <note>A reference to the filtration method described on the previous page, which some chemists falsely called distillation</note>, everything runs through the filter; yet it must be such a beautiful, special philosophical green **Spirit** <term>Spiritus: the volatile, gaseous, or distilled essence of a substance</term>. 

There have been others—and indeed the best among them all—who have remained loyal to the literal meaning of the word "distilling" and have sought it through the act of distillation. Because they could in no way produce such a green color from the **Vitriol** alone, they conjectured that the **Philosophers** <note>The ancient or esteemed alchemical authorities</note> had added something else to the **Vitriol** and had kept this secret from us. 

For this reason, using quick manual maneuvers, they drove the **Vitriol** and the **Saltpeter** <term>Salpeter: potassium nitrate, a key ingredient in making nitric acid</term> from two different **retorts** <term>Retorten: glass vessels with long, downward-pointing necks used for distilling liquids or gases</term> into a single **receiver** <term>Recipienten: the vessel that catches the distilled product</term>. There, these two **spirits** then worked into one another, and through their action upon and against each other, a green **liquor** or **spirit** resulted, at least in color. With this, they were satisfied. 

This is, of all methods, still the best **method** <note>original: *modus*</note>—not for the sake of its assumed greenness (which is only a reflection arising from the reaction of the two spirits, and is thus merely like a green color painted on from the outside), but because of the joining of these two spirits. From this union, a **Spirit** <insert>of</insert> very subtle, penetrating, and thinning power arises, through which it has a rapid effect; it is not to be despised in cases of **Epilepsy** <term>Epilepsia: historically called the "falling sickness"; volatile spirits were often used as smelling salts or internal medicine to treat seizures</term>.

<summary>The author critiques various methods for producing "philosophical vitriol," concluding that the best approach involves combining the distilled vapors of vitriol and saltpeter into one vessel, creating a potent medicinal spirit useful for treating epilepsy.</summary>

# Of the Secret

...but rather deserves its praise; and let no one imagine for themselves—as I also believed in my early ignorance and for that reason greatly feared this **spirit** <note>original: *spiritum*</note>—as if this were an **aqua fortis** <term>Aqua fortis: literally "strong water," the historical name for nitric acid, a highly corrosive mineral acid</term>, because **aqua fortis** is also made from **Vitriol** and **saltpeter**; not at all, for the matter of **aqua fortis** is quite different than is commonly supposed. Not the least bit of the power of the **Vitriol** can enter into the **aqua fortis**, because all its power and acidity, as soon as the **saltpeter** comes together with it and becomes warm, is fettered and bound by the **niter** <note>original: *nitro*; another name for saltpeter</note>, through the singular love that exists between the two of them. There would be much to say of this and it could be very clearly proven, but it cannot be so now for the sake of beloved brevity. 

Thus I say that it is impossible for anything from the **Vitriol** to pass over <note>i.e., to travel through the distillation neck into the receiver</term>, because its nature is changed in an instant as soon as the **niter** <note>original: *Nitrum*</note> is warmed with it. But because this seemingly green, highly volatile **Spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus*</note> consists of the true essential parts and spirits of the **Vitriol** and **niter**, it is of an entirely different nature than an **aqua fortis**, which is only a simple sour spirit of saltpeter. Therefore, it is not to be feared. I deemed it necessary to mention this to avoid [misunderstandings], and I will save the rest for another time. 

To say nothing now of other kinds of imagined distilled greens, as it would become too extensive to recount everything; these and all other errors have arisen solely and only from the fact that to the seekers of truth, the ground of the twofold or saturated...

# Of the Sulfur of the Philosophers.
<note>original: *Des Sulphuris Philosophorum*</note>

...saturated spirits have been unknown, without which the copper can never pass over into a **volatile liquid** <term>liquamen volatile: a liquid substance that has been rendered capable of evaporating or distilling</term>; if this foundation had been known until now, the true green **Spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus*</note> would have been discovered long ago, which I have then explained thoroughly enough in these two preceding chapters. This double corrosive agent now adheres to the copper—after it has made it into green, sweet **Vitriol** <term>Vitriol: a historical term for metal sulfates; "sweet" vitriol implies a purified or processed form</term>—not from the outside, like other corrosives, for that is the sole reason why the others cannot transform the copper: because they do not reach the copper’s innermost **Center** <note>original: *Centrum*</note>, but rather, like hungry, gluttonous spirits, they attack the metal from the outside and merely cling to it, as it were. 

Therefore it happens that when they are driven with a strong fire, they must leave their dwelling-place again, and the copper remains behind in the same state as before, seeing as it can soon be melted back into a solid body. But the double corrosive, as has already been mentioned many times, does not eat away at the metal, nor does it attack it with such hungry fury—much less does it only cling to it externally—but rather softens it through and through and penetrates to the very innermost part; indeed, it unites so firmly and deeply with the copper that it cannot leave it when it is driven by the fire. Instead, because this is a corrosive volatile spirit, the copper must pass over with it as a flying, volatile **Spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus*</note>, so much so that not even a single **grain** <note>original: *Gran*; a traditional unit of weight, roughly 0.06 grams</note>...

For this, time, labor, and patience are required, and nothing herein can be rushed; instead, understanding and no force must be used. One must diligently pay heed to the nature of things and how they wish to be driven, so that one does them justice and follows the path of nature. Such things you must learn from experience itself and from the nature of the things you have in hand; for here all the instruction that one man can give to another ceases. In this, we must only go into the "School of Nature" and sharpen our understanding upon the **subjects** <note>original: *subjecta*; the physical materials or chemical substances being worked upon</note> before us. Above all things, however, we must pray for success to come down from above, from our God and Father, in childlike simplicity and humility. Just as it has been reported here regarding **copper** <term>Kupffer: a base metal often associated with the planet Venus in alchemical symbolism</term>, so the matter also stands with **gold** <term>Golde: the "perfect" metal, associated with the Sun, which alchemists sought to purify or create</term>, which, after it [has been put] into its doubled and saturat-

# Of the Sulfur of the Philosophers

Because of the great similarity of both their natures, they both become one, and remain unchangeably and indissolubly one. In this [other] case, however—that is, in the violent dissolution—it is not so; for although it has the appearance of being a **radical solution** <note>original: *radicalis solutio*; a thorough or "root" dissolution where the substance is completely broken down</note>, the outcome proves that there is such a difference between this one and that one, and that they are as unequal to each other as the essence itself is to the essence’s shadow. 

This is because in this [violent] dissolution, the **solvent** <term>Solvens: the liquid medium, such as an acid, used to dissolve a solid substance</term> can be completely separated again from the **solute** <term>Soluto: the substance that has been dissolved into the liquid</term> through artificial manual techniques. Furthermore, the **dissolved matter** <note>original: *Solutum*</note>, if it were melted by a strong fire, would again become exactly what it was before it underwent the violent dissolution. 

In that other one, however—the natural dissolution—not only can the **solvent** never be separated from the **solute**, but the **solute** also becomes, through this **solution** <note>original: *solution*</note>, far more glorious, noble, and excellent, and incomparably higher in its power than it was before. In the violent **solution**, however, the dissolved substance remains in its previous nature and essence, and is only made ready for the true, essential, natural, and effective or thorough solution. 

And because this is a highly necessary **knot to be untied** <note>original: *nodus enodandus*; a difficult problem requiring a solution</note>—as almost all **beginners of chemistry** <note>original: *tyrones chymiatriæ*</note> are misled and shamefully deceived in this, taking violent **dissolutions** <note>original: *solutiones*</note> for natural ones, **delighting** <note>original: *delectiren*</note> and **contenting themselves** <note>original: *acquiesciren*</note> in them, thinking they have already grasped the truth of the matter,

# Of the Mystery

and thus do not bother with further investigation into the truly proper constitution of the true, natural dissolution, I must make this entirely clear and lay it out completely naked. This is so that eager seekers of truth are no longer deceived by the fraudulent, seductive, and lying "painted" processes with which all common printed chemical books are entirely full—nay, overflowing—and which are laboriously followed in vain by investigators of nature at great expense. Instead, they should open their own eyes, begin to see with their own eyes, and rightly observe and examine the nature of the things they have at hand, working in accordance with the whole of nature. They should no longer so easily fall for the sweet, lying processes of boastful writers <note>original: *Scribenten*</note>, nor spend all their fortune, precious time, blood-sweating labor, and great expense upon them.

# Of the Philosophers' Sulfur. 55

...passed over into a completely volatile spirit. This will seem to the ignorant to be a dissolution according to nature—and therefore henceforth inseparable—not only because it occurred so pleasantly, but also because it proves itself inseparable from its **solvent** <note>original: *Solvente*; the liquid used to dissolve a substance</note>. This is because the **solute** <note>original: *Solutum*; the substance that has been dissolved</note>, according to outward appearance and due to a particular harmony and equality of essence, follows its solvent even into the highest heights <note>meaning the dissolved metal remains joined to the liquid even when it is evaporated and distilled</note>. 

I must confess that this is such a subtle matter that it can deceive not only the <term>beginners <note>original: *tyrones*</note>: apprentices or those newly initiated into the chemical arts</term>, but even those who consider themselves highly experienced. It can lead them to believe that such a spiritual and volatile metallic vapor is the true <term>fifth essence <note>original: *quintam essentiam*</note>: the most concentrated, pure, and transformative "soul" of a substance</term>. 

Conversely, experience proves that if one throws <term>mercury <note>original: *Mercurium*</note>: quicksilver, used here as a reagent to test the stability of the solution</term> into such a supposed metallic fifth essence, everything that is metallic separates from the solvent. If one then brings this material into the smelting fire, it becomes the same metal once again as it was before. Thus, the reader sees that it was only the *appearance* of an essential or fundamental dissolution, and it added not the slightest change or improvement to the metal—unless it were maintained in its <term>solubility <note>original: *Solubilität*</note>: the state of being able to be dissolved or remain in liquid form</term> by a skillful hand and not returned to its previous hardness, as shall be taught hereafter. 

On the other hand, let the seeker of truth look upon **sulfur**, when it is dissolved in a distilled oil and its inward red <term>tincture <note>original: *Tinctur*</note>: the potent, concentrated medicinal or spiritual essence of a substance, often identified by its deep color</term> is turned outward, and thus

# Of the Secret. 56

...right down to the ground and the innermost root, you will see how firmly these two not only cling to one another—becoming one out of two—but also how they can never again be separated from each other by any manner or means. Not only that, but the **sulfur** <note>original: *Schwefel*; in alchemy, sulfur represents the "soul" or the principle of inflammability and color</note> becomes ten times more glorious, excellent, and powerful in its virtues than it was before. You may experience all of this in the following way:

**Dissolve** <note>original: *Solvire*</note> the combustible sulfur of common [sulfur], or of **antimony** <note>original: *antimonii*; a brittle, silvery-gray metalloid prized by alchemists for its purifying properties</note>, or of any other **subject** <note>original: *subjecti*; the base material being worked upon</note> in a distilled oil until the oil becomes quite thick or blood-red. Pour such a **solution** <note>original: *solution*</note> into a **retort** <note>a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling liquids</note> and draw the oil over very gently. The oil will pass over quite clear and white; however, when nothing clear will come over any longer, then strengthen the fire with power. Then the last of the oil arrives and brings the dissolved sulfur over with it in part as a red oil. 

Pour the clear oil that first passed over back onto the remaining dark-brown essence; **cohobate** <note>original: *cohobir*; the alchemical process of repeatedly pouring a distillate back onto its residue and re-distilling it to increase potency</note> it again, and repeat this so often until almost all of your **extract** <note>original: *extractum*</note> has passed over and all of your oil has climbed over, quite red and magnificent with **tincture** <note>the concentrated color or medicinal essence of a substance</note>. 

In this way, you have united your **solvent** <note>original: *Solvens*; the liquid used to dissolve</note> and **solute** <note>original: *Solutum*; the substance that was dissolved</note> with one another so that they can never be separated again by any art. This is because, due to the equality of their essence, they take such pleasure in one another and are so intimately united that one essence is formed from them both; thus, either both flow away together through the power of the fire...

Their supposed **oils**, **spirits**, or **metallic essences** <note>original: *essentiæ metallicæ*</note> are nothing else (as our Philosopher calls his previous follies regarding gold in his *Preliminary Exercises on Meteors* <note>original: *progymnasmate meteori*, § 6</note>) than a **deceptive essence** <note>original: *mentitus liquor*; literally a "lying liquid"</note>, which only has the appearance of an essential dissolution. In truth, it is nothing of the sort, because it can very soon be turned back into what it was before. A seeker of truth obscures himself significantly if he is captivated by such outward appearances and deceptive essences and remains stuck there.

### ...of the Sulfur of the Philosophers.

rather, it is only made convenient or skilled for further and subsequent dissolution, so that from such a state the true **soul** <note>original: *anima*</note> may be obtained through a true, thorough **solution** <term>solution: the alchemical process of "opening" a solid body into a liquid state to release its inner essence</term> that is in accordance with nature.

One should not deceive oneself into thinking they possess a treasure of health when it is actually only a shadow; indeed, such a supposed **metallic essence** <note>original: *essentia metallica*</note> can, in a short time—even a few hours—be turned back into the same crude metal it was before by a skilled hand. 

This is also acknowledged by the experienced **chemist** <note>original: *Chymiater*</note> and diligent investigator of nature, **Zwelfer** <note>Johann Zwelfer (1618–1668), an influential physician and chemist who critiqued standard pharmaceutical preparations of his day</note>, who confesses in his writings that the previously known **tinctures**, **essences**, etc., of metals are nothing other than **solutions** of the metallic **bodies** <note>original: *corporum*</note>, and by no means true separations of the **tincture** or the soul from the body.

Yet, as such a step is nevertheless highly necessary and must occur—provided we wish to truly enjoy the metallic **arcana** <term>arcana: secret, powerful medicinal preparations believed to contain the concentrated "virtue" or healing power of a substance</term> and intend to rely upon them for serious illnesses, next to God's blessing—we now proceed further and investigate how we may now obtain the pure **tincture** from this prepared and refined copper, since that is our sole objective...

# Of the Secret

and wish to separate; thus we find that our volatile copper is surrounded by a large quantity of a **double corrosive** <note>likely a mixture of two strong acids or salts used to break down the metal</note>, which must then necessarily be separated beforehand, if we wish to obtain the pure **soul** <note>original: *anima*; the vital essence or "sulfur" of the metal</note> without any foreign mixture. This is all the more necessary because the corrosive has already performed its task—namely, to tear the copper into the most subtle and volatile parts and to reduce it to the finest dust. 

Now, to separate this corrosive from the copper, one must use **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurium*</note> or other such volatile substances. Because these substances have a greater affinity or attraction to this double corrosive than the copper does, they unite with it immediately. And because they are also volatile, they tear this double corrosive away from the copper by force through a single **distillation** <note>the process of heating a liquid to create vapor and then condensing it back into liquid to purify it</note>, leaving the copper behind at the bottom as a coarse, dead, and re-hardened, useless powder. 

But this is not the right way at all! For in such a manner, all the labor spent on making the metal volatile would be done in vain, since such a dead powder is of the same nature as any other common **saffron of Venus** <term>crocus veneris: a chemical preparation of copper, usually an oxide or salt, named after Venus, the planetary association for copper</term>, such as is made by common **nitric acid** <term>aquafort: (aqua fortis) "strong water," a highly corrosive mineral acid</term> or **royal water** <term>aqua regis: a mixture of acids capable of dissolving even gold</term>. 

For the double corrosive has completely inverted the copper, turning its innermost parts outward and thus making it not only entirely convenient for extraction, but also, through this corrosive and its power, the sweet **soul** <term>anima: the essential, medicinal, or transformative spirit of the substance</term> of the copper...

# Of the Sulfur of the Philosophers.

as shines through by means of a waking and life-giving medium; likewise, without such a proper medium, this **tincture** <note>the medicinal or transformative essence of the metal</note> would not be able to radiate outward. Therefore, this **corrosive** <note>the acidic solvent used to break down the metal</note> must be withdrawn and separated from the copper very gradually and, as it were, imperceptibly, so that the copper may thus be turned outward with its "sweetness" <note>an alchemical term for the non-corrosive, restorative essence of a substance</note>, and may remain in a radiant property and a convenient state of **solubility**. 

This [solubility] cannot be maintained if this **corrosive**—by which the copper was, as it were, awakened from the dead—is snatched away and pushed aside all at once with force by **mercury** or its like. For in such a way, every **atom** <note>original: *atomus*; here referring to the smallest particle or unit of the metal</note> of the copper is closed up just as hard as before, because its awakening corrosive was withdrawn from it by force and all at once. 

Instead, one must use other suitable things which, firstly, carry away the corrosive not all at once and with force, but gradually, pleasantly, softly, and gently. Secondly, one must use things which do not precipitate the copper and separate it from its **double corrosive**, but which **dissolve** <note>original: *solviren*</note> both the copper and the corrosive together. Thus, even though in every **distillation** <term>cohobation: the process of repeatedly pouring a liquid back onto its solid residue and re-distilling it to refine the substance</term> some of the double corrosive passes away, the copper is **preserved** <note>original: *conserviren*</note> in its previous nature—namely, in a state of **solubility**. In this way, the copper and all its tiny dust particles [remain] in the same condition

It thus gradually lets the copper it held within itself drop away, as you will then see. 

In every cohobation, some of the metal will be found at the bottom as a very light, spongy dust. And if you should perhaps make a mistake—as has indeed happened to me at times—by driving the distillation too hard and making the fire too strong, then occasionally some of the metal tends to rise over the helm with such wonderful **colors** <note>original: *Colores*</note> that they cannot be gazed upon enough. These beautiful, joyful colors remain settled in the helm and stick to it continually, representing the most beautiful **peacock's tail** <term>peacock's tail: *cauda pavonis*, an alchemical stage where a shimmering, iridescent array of colors appears in the vessel, signaling a transition in the material</term>. # Of the Secret

[There is] no comparison between it <note>the common metallic oxide</note> and this one, for their powders <note>original Latin: "croci"</note> are dead bodies, but here is a body <note>original Latin: "corpus"</note> awakened from death and made alive again. This is because its soul is turned entirely outward and stands in full radiance, as you can also perceive from the magnificent splendor that this powder <term>crocus: a term used by alchemists for a fine metallic powder, usually an oxide, named for its saffron-like color</term> leaves behind in the helm <term>helm: the top part or "head" of a still (alembic) where vapors condense</term> with its most exquisite colors, should it happen to be driven over by an excessively strong heat. 

Through such outward-turned light, this powder now possesses such great power, both internally as a medicine and externally for all kinds of plasters, ointments, and other types of external remedies. # Of the Mystery

This doubled *spirit* <note>original Latin: "spiritus"</note> received from the golden *soul* <note>original Latin: "anima"</note> of copper—for although not the slightest speck of this golden tincture remained with this *corrosive*, it has nevertheless received a radiance from it, which it has kept within itself, and which stands as if deeply impressed upon it by a seal—has therefore become even more powerful than it was on its own before. Therefore, let this *hungry spirit of wine* <term>spiritum vini esurinum: a potent alchemical solvent made by distilling spirit of wine with sharp, "hungry" mineral salts or acids</term> be recommended to you in all *instances* where you are otherwise accustomed to using *acids* and *sharp solvents* <note>original Latin: "acida und esurina"</note>; you shall then experience what it is capable of against all unnatural heat, and how much you will have cause to rejoice in it over all known *acid spirits* <note>original Latin: "spiritus acidos"</note>. 

Furthermore, its great *corrosiveness* is very much softened and weakened by the *spirit of wine*, and is thus much safer to use than before. And because it is so closely united with the *spirit of wine*, it is very quickly joined with the *archeus* <term>archeo: in Paracelsian medicine, the "internal alchemist" or vital force of the body that directs healing and digestion</term> through it, and thus performs its work very swiftly; because the power of the *spirit of wine* is soon accepted by the body, the potency of this excellent *hungry vinegar* <note>original Latin: "aceti esurini"</note> simultaneously penetrates through and gently cools the heated spirits.

## The Eighth Chapter.

### In what manner the pure **soul** or **Tincture** must be extracted from the true **crocus of Venus**, and by what means such may be obtained.

<note>original: "anima"; "croco veneris". Crocus of Venus is a chemical term for a red or burnt powder derived from copper, often its oxide.</note>

After you have brought your **copper** so far that it is first divided into the very smallest **atoms** <note>original: "atomos"</note> through a process of volatilization, and torn into inconceivable dust particles by a flying, volatile, doubled power, and thereafter further entirely freed again from all **corrosive** by a soft, gentle way—then, and not before, is it fitting to surrender its tingeing **soul** and heavenly power to such a **menstruum** <term>menstruo: a solvent used in alchemy to dissolve or extract the essence of a substance</term> which stands in equality of essence with its **soul** <note>original Latin: "anima"</note>. Specifically, it must be a solvent that is of as fiery a nature as the **Tincture** of copper, which is a pure fire; for this reason, it is called the "element of fire" by our **Philosopher** <note>original: "Philosopho"</note> as well as by the other sages. Therefore, it can be extracted, separated, or thoroughly dissolved by nothing else than by a very fiery, penetrating, violent, and sharp-cutting volatile essence, as that which alone has the power—out of the hard bond of metallic **coagulation** which nature has accomplished in the copper—through a strong **magnetic force** of its like-

so, although a metal is ground and dusted ever so small, this fiery **solvent** <term>menstruum: a liquid substance used to dissolve others, here a specialized alchemical preparation</term> cannot take its likeness to itself, because that [inner nature] has not yet been turned outwards again, nor has it been brought from its hard **thickening** <term>Coagulation: the state of being solidified or clotted</term> into a spongy, loose lightness. This volatilization is to be obtained in no other way than through double spirits; for which reason the **Philosophers** <note>the ancient and medieval alchemical authorities</note> have kept this preparation of **copper** <note>original: "Kupffers"</note> just as secret as the extraction and separation of the **Tincture** <term>Tinctur: the concentrated essence or "medicine" of the metal</term> from the **body**. 

They did this because they knew that one without the other would be of little use; namely, that one cannot use the volatilized **alkali** <term>alcali: a basic salt, often derived from ashes</term> without the volatilized **powder** for any extraordinary benefit, and conversely, the **powder** without the volatile **alkali** would not yield its power. Thus, both **operations** <term>operationes: the specific steps or procedures in an alchemical work</term> were kept by them in equal secrecy. Pour out the dissolved liquid, and pour fresh **spirit** upon the remainder. Let it **dissolve** <note>original: *solviren*</note> again as before, and continue this process until nothing more can be extracted from the **saffron**, and your poured-on fiery **solvent** <term>menstruum: a liquid used to extract or dissolve solids</term> can take up no more. Then, pour all these **solutions** together and very gently distill your fiery spirit back over again, which you may thereafter use for similar...

# Of the Secret
